# Changes and scan evidence

Counts are decision places, not individual character edits. Against C/H counts substantive differences of reading or placement; certainty alone is excluded from those two counts. Rows additionally includes cropped resolutions of C doubts even when their selected reading is unchanged. Added doubt alone is listed in the doubts file. Pure macro differences and H editorial notes are excluded. Confidence qualifies the reading at that place.

## Consequential corrections

- 145_scan028, L10 overworked tau: `\dot\tau` — A distinct small dark dot stands above the second tau's retraced upper bar, and the bent stem with lower return remains a readable tau. The retracing does not obscure either identifying feature. Resolve the first-pass local doubt to dotted tau. The first arrow has a left bar, and the second has a right arrowhead; arrow-length macro choices do not change the reading. No independent canceled t is recoverable within the retracing.
- 145_scan028, L10 second arrow: `\to` — The second arrow has a pointed head and a short upturned start, unlike the clearly extended vertical tail on the first mapsto. There is no clear full tail bar on the second arrow.
- 145_scan028, L14 second stability subscript: `\Gamma_2\subset\Gamma` — The subscript is a single 2 with an upper curve and low foot. There is no numerator 1 or fraction stroke. H explicitly gives 1/2 by inference; the ink says 2 and is retained.
- 145_scan029, Top insertion: dot over tau: `\{1,\dot\tau\}` — A dark isolated dot lies immediately above the tau crossbar. It is comparable in darkness and size to the dots over sigma elsewhere on the page, and is not a paper speck.
- 145_scan029, Locally hatched first generator: `\deleted{\unsure{\rho'|\dot\rho'}}` — The loop and descender suggest rho, but several short dark diagonal hatches obscure the upper part and any prime/dot. These local hatches are distinct from the long diagonal block cancellation. The underlying symbol is not resolved; a separate local deletion is recorded.
- 145_scan029, Product defining first generator: `\rho\unsure{\tau|\deleted{\tau}}` — After the equality, rho has an open loop and long hooked descender. The following symbol retains the crossbar and curved lower stroke of tau despite the large diagonal strike crossing the block. I do not find a separate local cancellation of this tau beyond the block cancellation. Root retains the uncertainty about an additional local cancellation in place; the legible tau is first.
- 145_scan029, Cancelled mark immediately before first decorated sigma: `\deleted{\unsure{\tau}}` — A small dark, locally crossed symbol stands between the comma and the first decorated sigma. Its bar and lower hook support tau, but overtracing prevents a firm reading. Its presence and cancellation are clear.
- 145_scan029, First relation: indices of the involutions: `\widetilde{\dot\sigma}_1^2=\widetilde{\dot\sigma}_2^2=\widetilde{\dot\sigma}_3^2=1` — The first subscript is a single upright 1, not a closed 0. The second has a rounded upper turn and short lower exit, a compressed 2 rather than the long upright used for 1. The third has the two connected turns of 3. The surrounding list uses 0,1,infinity, but the written 1,2,3 in the struck relation are preserved.
- 145_scan029, Second relation: obscured rho and exponent: `\unsure{{\rho'}^{\deleted{-1}2}|\dot\rho^{-2}}` — The ending has rho with a small upper mark and several dark overwritten exponent strokes, ending in a clear 2. A former -1 under the overtracing is possible, but neither it nor the prime versus overdot position is fully secure. C's unresolved alternatives are retained; the entire product relation was reread on the crop.
- 145_scan030, Lower left note, tildes and connector: `\tilde\sigma_0,\tilde\sigma_1,\tilde\sigma_\infty\ \longrightarrow` — Three separate wavy strokes stand above the sigmas. A rightward shaft with arrowhead points from this note to the fourth row of the principal system. They are not dots or optional editorial decoration.
- 145_scan030, Right bracketed note, canceled first l_1 formula: `\deleted{l_1=\tau_0\tau_\infty}` — The l loop, the vertical subscript 1, two equality strokes, tau_0 and tau_infinity remain independently readable across the diagonal cancellation. The comma after the formula is outside the cancellation.
- 145_scan030, Right bracketed note, canceled l_infinity starter: `\deleted{\unsure{l_\infty=}}` — Above the last tau of the second live row is a separately canceled starter. The l loop and equals sign are visible; the small subscript under the canceling strokes remains doubtful. It is not omitted.
- 145_scan030, Lower right note, disputed verb: `\unsure{conjuguent|engendrent}` — The initial small curved letters, a descending loop and final nt are visible, but the middle joins are compressed and overlap the descender from the line above. The crop does not securely distinguish conjuguent from engendrent; neither the grammar nor group-theoretic expectation is used to force it.
- 145_scan030, Lower right note, ss-groupe engendré: `ss-groupe engendré` — The next baseline starts with a compact doubled s-form and a dash. Groupe and engendré follow, with the g loops and the final acute stroke visible. No separate un or written sous precedes groupe.
- 145_scan031, First derived relation, left underbrace: `\sigma_0\underbrace{\rho\sigma_0^{-1}\rho^{-1}}_{\sigma_1}` — The left hook begins below rho, to the right of the initial sigma_0. The right hook ends beneath rho^{-1}; sigma_1 is below the resulting three-factor group. The initial sigma_0 is outside the brace.
- 145_scan031, Final words and punctuation: `conserver le centre!` — Conserver has the low c/on and s/e/r/v/e/r sequence. The article is le without s. Centre begins c/e/n, has one tall crossed t, and ends r/e without s. No comma occurs before the exclamation mark.
- 145_scan033, Formula (2): overwritten first subscript: `g_{\deleted{\unsure{0}}\infty}` — The first g and its lower-right area are visibly retraced, with an earlier rounded mark under the final infinity. The earlier 0 is not fully separable and is retained as doubtful deleted ink. H's editorial note reports this correction, so the state is not counted as an H omission.
- 145_scan033, M0888: last g subscript in (2): `\unsure{\infty|0}` — The compact subscript is a horizontal oval with a dark middle crossing and what appear to be two lobes. It resembles infinity more than a clean single 0, but overtracing prevents a secure decision. Infinity leads the retained alternatives; mathematical expectations do not settle it.
- 145_scan033, M0890: last sigma argument in (2): `\unsure{\infty|0}` — The small argument has a dense oval/crossing similar to the final g subscript rather than an unmistakable simple zero. The outer sigma parentheses are visible. Infinity is the leading visual reading, but zero remains possible under the retracing.
- 145_scan033, M0891: overwritten sign in first row of (3): `\deleted{\mapsto}=` — A fainter mapsto bar and right arrowhead extend past two shorter, darker horizontal equality shafts. The earlier arrow and the equality laid over it are both recorded. H's note correctly supplies this state and is treated as agreement.
- 145_scan033, M0893: group in multiplication sentence: `\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal E}}` — Between dans and est there is one calligraphic E carrying two hats. The blank immediately to its left contains no S. The larger group S-double-hat-E in the title and maps is not silently restored in this sentence.
- 145_scan034, (9′), second coordinate of right-hand tuple: `\sigma` — The right-hand entry has the horizontal head and small lower loop of sigma, visibly different from the upright 1 in the left-hand tuple. The preceding sigma=1 instruction does not license replacing the written sigma.
- 145_scan035, generator list: dot over sigma0: `\dot\sigma_0` — A small dark dot stands distinctly above the sigma carrying subscript zero. The subsequent sigma1 and sigma-infinity have no such dots.
- 145_scan035, target after se relèvent: `S\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal E}}` — Two separate chevrons are stacked above the E, both visible on the crop.
- 145_scan035, Gamma exponent before arrow to E: `\Gamma^+\to\mathcal E` — The superscript has one upright crossing one horizontal, with no oblique arms. It differs visibly from the compact, multi-arm star in Gamma_P*. The entire short Gamma-to-E formula was checked on the crop.
- 145_scan036, Overwritten initial group factor: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathbb Z}}\inserted{\{\pm1\}}` — The overworked factor has a Z-like diagonal and lower bar underneath; that cancelled letter is not fully separable and remains unsure. Braces, plus/minus strokes and the upright 1 form the replacement before the multiplication sign. H leaves the underlying group illegible and omits the replacement. Its note acknowledging cancellation is not counted as a separate deletion-state error.
- 145_scan036, End of group chain and beginning of following sentence: `\Gamma, on voit que` — There is a comma after Gamma, followed on the next line by on voit que. No closing parenthesis or additional il n’est autre que Gamma_P clause intervenes. Crop 09 also shows the immediate continuation on voit que.
- 147_scan003, entry4: théorie: `théorie` — After théo the crop has a short r, dotted i and final e; it lacks the extra rounded m strokes required by théorème. The accent over é is visible.
- 147_scan003, entry6: générisation: `générisation` — The middle proceeds through the short r/i/s sequence into -ation; no tall l or added -al- occurs. The g descender, accent marks, t cross and -ion ending are visible.
- 147_scan003, entry7: whole subscript of hatted M: `\widehat M_{\unsure{g,\nu|g}}` — The subscript has a looping lower g-like body and long descender. A short curved trace at its right is visible, but comma and nu cannot both be isolated confidently. The whole subscript is therefore marked doubtful. It is not certified from the title, and H's capital G is too categorical. The hat, M and adjacent inequality were inspected together.
- 147_scan003, entry9: variables: `variables` — Initial v/a, short r and dotted i are followed by a tall looped b, tall l and -es. There is no p descender or crossed t corresponding to compactes. All letters of variables are visible.
- 147_scan003, entry9: cas transcendant: `cas transcendant` — A separate cas precedes the next tall initial t. The second word has trans-cend-ant, with a tall d and a terminal crossed t; the final -tion required by construction is absent. The comma at right remains.
- 147_scan003, entry9: générisation: `générisation` — The short middle r/i/s strokes lead directly toward -ation, without an added -al-. The visible accents and t/i/on ending agree with the earlier générisation form.
- 147_scan003, entry10: action: `action` — The short initial a/c strokes lead to one tall t, dotted i, low o and terminal n. The two tall l forms expected in calcul are absent.
- 147_scan003, entry10: normalisation: `normalisation` — The beginning is a low n/o/r/m run rather than a looped descending g. The subsequent a/l/i/s/a/t/i/o/n strokes are present. No initial accent or g descender appears.
- 147_scan007, L13 relation identifying A: `A\simeq\widetilde A/\sigma` — The upper stroke of the relation is visibly wavy, with a rise and dip, while the lower stroke is straight. This is the written simeq, not a pair of straight equality bars.
- 147_scan007, Diagonal note, predicate: `ce foncteur est` — After ce foncteur, the next diagonal baseline starts with the low e/s strokes and a tall crossed t. There is no preceding n or apostrophe. The word is est, not n’est.
- 147_scan007, Diagonal note, compatibility phrase and M0924: `compatible aux images inverses` — Compatible has a rounded initial c/o, joined m/p strokes, a tall t and the following i/b/l/e sequence. Aux shows a low a/u sequence followed by a two-stroke x; this resolves M0924. Images has a dotted i and long g descender; inverses has dotted i, low n/v/e and the final r/s/e/s sequence. The words are present on this sheet.
- 147_scan008, diagram (8): horizontal arrow label: `o` — The label is a closed rounded oval with a short curved exit rising to the right. The quotient sigma immediately below has a much narrower angular loop under a long straight horizontal top stroke. This same-page contrast favors o. Root independently reports four matching o labels on 147_scan009; reader A did not inspect that page. All three arrowheads, labels p and q, objects and the quotient were checked with the whole diagram.
- 147_scan009, Opening paragraph, Les applications o et p: `$o$` — The named map is a small closed oval with an upper-right exit flick. On the same crop the involution sigma has a broader angular bowl and a long straight top bar. The oval form recurs in both arrow labels and after l'application.
- 147_scan009, First system, horizontal arrow label (M0929): `o` — Whole tuple checked. The label has a closed narrow loop with a rightward exit stroke; it matches the small o in the opening sentence, whereas sigma_tildeA at the right of this same tuple has an angular bowl and a long upper bar. The exit stroke can look sigma-like in isolation; the repeated local forms support o.
- 147_scan009, Second system, horizontal arrow label (M0930): `o` — Whole two-row system checked. Above the one-shaft right arrow is a closed slanted loop with an upper-right exit stroke, matching the opening map name and the first arrow label. The degree label is deg 2; the downward arrow ends at A.
- 147_scan009, Origin-map name in running text (M0931): `o` — After l'application the symbol is a closed small oval with a short upper-right exit stroke. It matches the map name in the opening sentence and the arrow labels, and is followed by étant l'. The full surrounding parenthesis and formula-bearing line were checked.
- 147_scan014, Slanted upper-left marginal note, including M0934: `c'est le nb de pts à l'infini de la composante $X_\alpha$` — The clockwise-rotated crop shows three slanted lines: c'est le nb de / pts à l'infini de la / composante X_alpha. The disputed de has a looped d followed by a long e-tail, matching de before la on the next line; no separate s is visible. The other words are readable, including the p-descender in composante and the final X_alpha, which belongs to the note rather than to an extra main-line word. The reduced à has a short body below its detached accent.
- 147_scan014, Nested decorations in Y equals tilde-hat X: `Y=\widetilde{\widehat X}` — The broad wavy tilde is the upper decoration. Beneath it is a separate angular hat immediately above X. The complete Y_alpha de Y = ... formula and its point are visible.
- 147_scan016, raised parenthesis after h_1=0: word and attachment: `\inserted{(i.e. $G$ est un arbre)} after $h_1=0$` — The ending has the a/r onset, a tall b, then r/e; it is arbre, not cercle. The curved leader from the right parenthesis descends beside h_1=0, while the first h_1=1 lies above and to the left. The entire second-case formula and note are in the crop.
- 147_scan016, stability insertion: de type and numeric pair: `de type $(1,0)$` — After stable, the short de is followed by type, with the tall t and descending y/p. The pair (1,0) is written on a lower small baseline: the parentheses, comma and two numerical positions are visible through the leader. This is not d’après followed by physically illegible text.
- 147_scan016, condition after h_1=1: qui n’a pas de: `qui n'a pas de` — The tall descending q and the following u/i are visible at the right end of one line, then n, apostrophe and a. The next line begins pas de, with the p descender, joined a/s and d/e. The phrase spans two baselines and is not part of the pt. insertion.
- 147_scan018, Base subscript of N in the inserted tuple map: `\mathbb N_{\mathcal S}` — The target is a double-stroked N with a small curled S subscript. The upper loop and hooked lower return match the base letter in the G and Gamma subscripts and differ from the plain vertex S forms elsewhere in the tuple. This is an independently written subscript, unlike the unresolved overlap after sur.
- 147_scan018, Base letter in the insertion de S: `\inserted{de $\mathcal S$}` — De is followed by a narrow tall curled base letter with a hooked lower return. The insertion stroke attaches after étales and before et de morphismes. Its S belongs to the same base notation as the independently clear lower base S.
- 147_scan018, Base subscript in the constant graph G_S: `G_{\mathcal S}` — The subscript has a looped upper bowl and a sweeping hooked lower return, matching the base letter, not the plain vertex S. The main G is not underlined; the following family G is independently underlined.
- 147_scan018, Base subscript in Gamma_S-torseur: `\Gamma_{\mathcal S}` — The subscript under Gamma has the same curled upper and lower strokes as the base in G_mathcalS. It is distinct from the plain S in alpha in S at the foot of the page.
- 147_scan018, Noun after the disjoint-union formula: `l'ensemble` — The long word has compressed ensem strokes followed by the two tall b/l ascenders and a final e. Those ascenders, the longer middle group and the absence of a terminal t support ensemble rather than élément. The initial l and apostrophe are crowded but belong to the same word.
- 147_scan019, first connectedness qualifier: written digit and hyphen: `$\unsure{6|0}$-connexe` — The first digit has a long entry stroke rising above a closed lower loop, favoring the written shape 6; the later 0-connexes has a short round oval without that tall entry. A zero written with an unusually long entry remains possible. The preferred uncertainty is therefore 6|0, without imposing the expected connectedness value. H omits the numeral and hyphen altogether. The whole parenthetic condition was inspected in B02 and in the existing relt crop.
- 147_scan021, Opening, words before un iso can.: `de sorte qu'on aura` — The d loop and e are followed by s, low o/r, a tall t and terminal e in sorte. The next q has a long descender, then a raised apostrophe and on; aura follows as a separate word. These are visible words, not the shorter phrase in H.
- 147_scan021, Opening, after can.: `(nothing)` — A distinct point ends can.; the following space contains no word près. No stroke sequence for p-r-è-s follows the abbreviation.
- 147_scan022, Upper-left marginal note, word after the formula: `anabélien` — The rotated crop shows a-n-a, the tall b, accented é, l, dotted i, and the final e-n return. All letters belong to a separate second slanted marginal line.
- 147_scan023, Quoted noun: `poids` — After the long p stem and the o/i traces, the letter before the final s has a rounded ascending loop, the local d form. There is no separate n followed by a tall crossed t. The written noun is poids.

### Independent image-only second reading

Reader blind_c; 16 boxes read, 8 token-level differences adjudicated, 3 places changed. The second reading was written before comparison. Every mismatch below was inspected on its second-pass crop, including notational or scope differences that do not change the ink reading. Target boxes retain the frozen coverage coordinates; actual crop bounds and rotation describe the saved second-pass crop. Both sets of bounds use native scan coordinates.
